10. New Business 
Action 
a. Increasing New & Existing Revenues 
I. Athletics A slideshow presentation of athletic user fees is presented to increase fee for high school up 
to $325 and $275 for Middle School.  A 25% discount for siblings.  Reduced fee for high school to $50 
and $40 for middle school with a family cap of $1500.  Mr. Bigelow is asked to suggest a sport to cut   
Ms. Archambault makes a motion to accept the increase, Mr. Sculimbrene seconds the motion all 
approve via roll call vote 
II. Student Activities a proposal for an increase in fees, potential of having a base fee, and then 
additional fees based on clubs or activities students participate in.  Committee recommends Dr. Gilson 
meet with students to discuss these possibility of increases in fees. 
III. Transportation Bus Fee A proposal is presented to charge families a fee per student that ride the bus 
in grades 7-12 and also K-6 students that are under 2 miles from the school.  Committee would like a 
survey to go out to parents to get community feedback. 
IV. Facilities Proposal to increase fees by 50% Mr. Beardmore makes a motion to accept this as 
proposed, Mr. Sculimbrene seconds the motion, all approve via roll call vote.  
       Discussion 
b. Joint meeting with Select Board 3/11 The select board chair reached out trying to get a joint meeting with the 
select board and school committee to try and discuss anything and everything.    Committee agrees this is a 
good idea and will propose the March 11th date for the meeting 
c. Protection of Students & Their Information Lately there has been concern of access of personal information.  
We have had a recent breach however, we do not share any personal information or school records.  That 
information is private. 
 
11. Old Business 
Discussion 
a. FY26 Budget Update Slides are posted and available on the website. Dr. Burnham shares the presentation and 
explains how budgets are developed.  The administrators for the district explain their list of priorities to the 
committee. 
b. FY26 Budget Q &A  
Q  Laura Peria, 44 Whiting Street I have a kindergartener, who is enrolled at the Primary School and I also had two high 
school students already go through the Middle High.  I have seen all the different levels.  Everything said today was spot on  
and I appreciate all of your comments.  One question I had was you were talking about these meta-analysis studies, which 
again I think there is a lot of different studies out there, do you know what the range of classroom size that was studied?   I 
don’t think obviously there is an end point that classroom size may cut off.  I don’t think anyone is going to argue that a 
===PAGE 6===
 
1025 Massachusetts Avenue, Lunenburg, Massachusetts 01462 
978 582-4100 ~ fax 978 582-4103 
classroom size of 5 is the same as 5.  Do you know the actual range that was studied?  A.  Dr. Bookis responds A meta-
analysis which looked at many studies and they pinpointed that the impact on achievement was from 13 to 17 students. For 
high school from that point up to 30 students and middle school up to 28 there showed there was no negative effects of 
learning based on the class size.  Q.  Can you clarify what the current proposed cuts to MTSS are?  I noticed when I looked at 
the excel spreadsheet positions were grouped under Special Ed.  A.  Dr. Burnham answers all three positions were to be cut 
in the initial proposal.   Q. Has physical classroom size been assessed for the increase potentially in students, and is it 
appropriate and safe?  A.  Ms. Squier answers that we can fit more kids in the classrooms, however the materials that would 
be out on display and available will be limited.  Teachers will have to rearrange their classroom and be limited in their 
materials. Q. I saw a salary increase of $71,000  for an HR Specialist, I am curious as to what that represents and especially if 
we are cutting jobs why need to further HR support and do we have multiple HR positions?  A. Dr. Burnham shares that is 
not a new position, it was moved from a different part of the budget and no we do not have multiple positions Q. I noticed on 
the expense cuts that were presented tonight the additional cuts to PE and music programs in the lower levels, was that just 
materials? A. Yes 
Laura Barooshian 389 Mulpus Road Q. Can you repeat where the slide deck is online A. It is under the school committee 
link and then the Meeting Minutes and Agendas. Q. On the line items noted for music positions that was materials correct?    
A.  Yes that is materials not positions.  Q.  Some topics coming up were extra-large study halls, getting work credit, possible 
work credits, large PE classes were there other things considered but tabled? A.   No we expect that there will be an increased 
number of kids in study halls, all classes will be larger.  We are piloting a work a work based program, and will report back  at 
the end of the school year. Q.  I do like the idea of sponsorships, I think there are opportunities to have other funds coming 
in.  What is the plan for a longer term, 3 or 5 year plans?  A.  I do not have a three to five year plan, it is going to come down 
to a shift in a funding mechanism, I can tell you that if this is what it is we will make cuts this year and we will be back here a 
year from now with likely more cuts. 
Mr. Beardmore comments we are in a slightly different position then we were a year ago.  There are actually more knowns 
today.  A year ago we had several unsettled contracts that are now settled, including our contract with the LEA, those are big 
numbers and we can now project those costs out over the next two years.  The numbers that are going to be projected are not 
good, there are going to be more gaps.  We absolutely need to deal with and we need to have a very straight forward and 
realistic conversation with our partners on the select board, town manager, and or legislatures.  
Vi-Anne Brown 118 Whiting Street I have been a resident here since 2008.  I have two daughters in the school system.  I 
am also the school social worker at the Primary School.  In a town like Lunenburg that is small and highly interconnected, I 
do not think that our solution for the school is taking from another entity, because the all matter.  They all contribute to the 
school, they all contribute to the public safety of the town, and they all contribute to the library, and all the other vario us 
organizations that we have.  It all matters.  I think the CPA money would impact the school, my question is: What has 
prevented the town from moving forward with this?  A There have been two attempts, once it got to Town Meeting and lost, 
the other time the select board did not want to put it on the warrant.  (Comment)  We know learning cannot happen without a 
relationship.  The relationship doesn’t necessarily have to be with people, they can be with a specific subject matter or 
specific historian.  Typically with younger children it is a relationship with a caregiver.  I want everyone for a moment to 
think back on your most pivotal educational experiences. Did the building you were in matter, did anything about the grounds 
or the physical aspect about the environment matter?  Likely not.  What mattered? So Lunenburg, we ar e a community here 
and we actually get to make the decisions.  This is not about the school committee, this is not about the select board, although 
they are important, it is about all of us.  I would implore you, when you are making your decisions, whether  to vote or make a 
public comment, to think about what mattered to you and your educational experience.  I was also very fond of the 
sponsorship program, on thing that has been super impressive to me about this community is the willingness for people to 
just roll up their sleeves and do what needs to be done.  The volunteers in this community are exceptional, from the pasta 
parties for athletics to the families opening their houses and garages for an entire month in the fall to let the students bu ild 
floats. Q What are the limitations that currently exist in allowing parents or other volunteers to come in and support the 
school community?  What would we need to do to get through the barriers? A We have a flyer on the website with different 
volunteer opportunities and we do not get a lot of volunteers.  You don’t typically get as many volunteers for grades 6 -12 as 
you might for the Primary School or Turkey Hill.  We also have limitations, as information is confidential and a non -
employee would not be able to access to those kinds of things.  Otherwise we would in need of cori checks, it would be best 
for them to reach out to the PTO.   
Amy King 3 Gates Place Q There has been a lot of discussion about the athletics and the increase in the budget.  We see 
many towns near us that have various advertising on their fields to help support the athletic budgets.  I am wondering that h as 
been discussed and if we are looking at that as a source of revenue. A  Currently we have a school committee policy that 
prohibits this.  The school committee will be reviewing this policy.  Q I heard the conversation about increasing facilities 
fees, one concern I would have is are we also cutting facilities personnel but increasing those fees?  We really need to mak e 
sure we are maintaining the facilities. I know we had a big volunteer group in the fall/late summer that came and cleaned up a 
lot of the facilities and fields.  A None of the personnel cuts that would be connected to the use of the field or the gym are 
going to be impacted, they are not among the proposed cuts. Q You mentioned health insurance went up, is that with the 
current roster of personnel? A What is built into the budget is based on the January enrollment when we made cuts and pulled 
===PAGE 7===
 
1025 Massachusetts Avenue, Lunenburg, Massachusetts 01462 
978 582-4100 ~ fax 978 582-4103 
individuals out of the budget that were also taking health insurance. Q The large presentation on MTSS, have we looked at 
how other school districts are doing some of that?  Are there other districts not using coaches and having good results? A The 
coach is not limited to MTSS in terms of direct service to students.  The coach is a little bit of curriculum planning, 
prioritizing data, and differentiating instruction working directly with teachers.  The MTSS structures that we have in place  is 
a couple of licensed teachers working as intervention teachers, we have also had tutors.  I think this is the standard practice 
that build into your system.  You have staff to provide the direct service.  The goal for the coaches is to be working with t he 
teachers to strengthen the instruction that happens every single day.  If we can strengthen the instruction that is going on 
every single day in every single class, you have kids learning and you are not having as many kids that are having difficulty  
accessing the learning and therefore, in need of additional supports.  
Mr. Beardmore makes a motion to close the public portion of the Q&A, Mr. Sculimbrene seconds the motion all 
approve 
 
Mr. Lehtinen shares his list of priorities for the committee to discuss and provide feedback, Direc tor of Facilities and 
Grounds, it is recommended that Dr. Burnham hire the candidate she has interviewed.   A discussion about sharing resources 
with North Middlesex, which was not supported by the committee. Committee does not support eliminating the Direc tor of 
Teaching and Learning position, reducing Director of Special Services to part time  or eliminating all assistant principals or 
eliminating Athletic Director.  Committee tables the idea of eliminating Director of Finance and Administration and add an 
accountant position and HR Consulting Contract Support.  Committee supports eliminating one of the assistant principals at 
the Middle High School and the part time athletics secretary, making the IT tech to a part time position.  Committee goes 
through the expense cut proposal and discusses options. 
 
c. Superintendent Search Committee discusses timeline and ultimately decide to proceed as listed. 
  
12. Reports Tabled for the next meeting 
 
13. Public Comment Laura Peria Whiting Street, We started this meeting talking about priorities being classroom 
learning, instructional learning.  I would urge the committee and the superintendent to keep that in mind as you are 
looking at the budget.  I think I heard Tony say a few times, we are not cutting enough and yet these are all really hard 
decisions and nobody wants to cut athletics, nobody wants to raise fees, nobody wants to cut transportation, but I would 
just ask that we try to keep in mind serving the most students, our most vulnerable stu dents, serving that early education 
that we talked about being so critical as priority.  That is not going to make everyone happy.  I would really urge people 
to look at those transportation fees, I think that we went over a little quickly.  I agree with L aura though saying that may 
target certain groups more, but we also don’t want to raise fees.  I’m imploring the group to really think about our 
priorities.  It bothers me that I see a disproportionate cut at the K-6 levels in the classroom with the MTSS tutors and the 
teachers.  I would love to see that be a priority for restoring those positions.  I also appreciate lookin g at the admin roles, 
I think that is hugely helpful. 
 
Kara Harunkiewicz, 165 Gilchrest Street, I am speaking on behalf of myself.  As far as the student activities is 
concerned, when I looked at the list, I personally participate in 6 of them.  That would cost $450 which is more than any 
of the athletic fees were raised.  Two of them are directly related to music, which is also another hu ge expense.  I am 
trying to raise the awareness, even though some things that we are providing are seen as they don’t anything.  Any 
student in band has spent an insane amount of money to be in the band. Instruments cost a lot, you need reeds and that 
can cost $50 a semester.  It is not like those kids aren’t paying anything, they just aren’t paying anything directly to the 
school. 
 
Mr. Beardmore It has been a hard night, I need to say something that is even harder.  Tomorrow morning I will be 
informing the town clerk and the select board that I am resigning from the school committee effective May 16 th.  I spoke 
with the town clerk yesterday and the mechanics of this work such that, if I make my resignation known within a certain 
amount of time prior to the election, the position can be made available on the ballot at the next election.  My personal 
circumstances have changed rather dramatically since I first joined the school committee.  I have gotten married, my 
family has expanded quite a bit.  My family responsibilities have changed and I have recently taken on a new job.  That 
job is taking up a lot more of my time then my previous job.  I need to manage my responsibilities better and as a result 
of those changes, I don’t feel like I am personally able to put everything that I want to into the school committee.  I have 
certainly felt that over the course of the las several weeks and certainly through the budget season, I have done a fraction 
this year with respect to the budget that I did last year and the year before that.  I think I personally, my family, my 
colleagues and our school department deserve a member that can put more into this then I am currently able to.  So we 
will have three vacancies on the school committee in May.  I think that we also need to look over the schedule and see 
what needs to be done over the course of the next few months.  I don’t intend to retire from the public service, I will be 
back, but I do need to take a break it will be a little but I don’t know how long.

The minutes lists all the topics which may be discussed at the meeting and are those 
reasonably anticipated. Votes may be taken as a result of these discussions. Not all items 
listed may in fact be discussed and other items not listed may also be brought up for 
discussion to the extent permitted by Open Meeting Law. 
 
1. Call to Order-Call to order at 5:00pm by Mr. Lehtinen, seconded by Ms. Archambault.  
 
2. Public Comment-Brian introduced Alicia from MASC as our Field Director. 
 
3. Orientation with MASC Field Director: 
 a. Open Meeting Law-Ms. Mallon discussed the quorum of our committee which is 7 
based on the number of members(12).  Important everyone attends all meetings.  
Mentioned ethics law and respecting executive session-members prohibited from 
talking about any topics discussed in executive session outside of this room.  We are a 
confidential committee and will need to sign a confidentiality statement today. If you 
have a question, can ask Ms. Mallon directly.  
 
Conflict of interest- if there is someone you recognize, related to, etc., have to sign 
ethics document.  Cannot ask a candidate questions about age, race & ethnicity, 
religion, gender, health, disability.   
 
Role of Chair-chair has no more influence over body, one vote.  Help keeps committee 
===PAGE 2===
on track, making motions, posting meeting & minutes.  Start in open session & go into 
executive session to narrow semi-finalists next meeting.  
  
Typically have 8-10 interview applicants. Those meetings will be in executive session, 
not shared with other semifinalists.  Led by MASC rep.  Screening committee has to 
send at least two, maybe three as recommendation to SC.  No more than four. 
Committee can choose how many.  Internal candidates can be given courtesy of 
screening committee interview.  
 
Do’s & Don’t’s- Come into with no preconceived notions. Wide range of applicants, 
keep open mind.  Question as to what happens if we don’t have qualified candidate at 
end of process?  Ms. Mallon said that MASC will come back out and do another 
search for no cost.   
 
Discussed key words/themes important for our district: Budget theme, inclusive, 
communication theme-tone, receptive & outward), community supporter, school 
safety, leader, families, understands needs of district (size, makeup), visibility, honest, 
collaboration (town partnership), data driven,  receptive to feedback.   
 
Homework: think about survey and booklet.  Send three questions, MASC will merge 
and compile into interview questions.  
Will go through questions with group, fine tune.  Ms. Archambault said she will send 
listening session’s feedback to compile for group.  
 
Posting closes March 21st.  Three searches running concurrently-Beverly, Monson, etc.  
Already have applications coming in.  After deadline closes, committee will meet 
again to finalize questions and choose semi-finalists.  Group decides how many to 
bring in, MASC contacts candidates.  Exaample-9 people- 3 people 3 nights.   
 
MASC will send rubric and we will have our own notes from this to formulate top 
candidates.  
After this is sent to School Committee, finalists have a site visit in district, meet with 
admin, tour buildings, meet with teachers, dinner break.  Will have interview with 
School Committee in public at night.   
 
Will have QR code for feedback.     
 
Scheduling-Meet Tuesday the 25th at 5pm to go over questions, pick semifinalists.  Try 
for later that week or next? Ms. Archambault mentioned this falls when she is away.  
Said that another committee member would jump in if necessary. Another SC member 
would have to do all meetings if this was decided so process was not broken.   The 27th 
6:30pm & 28th   5pm & 31st,  6:00pm suggested.  One member gone after 30th, asked 
about virtual.  District visits not until after April Break. 
 
Ms. Foyle suggested interviews in Library classroom, exit right from there to side door 
for parking lot, and more private.   
 
A week from today is deadline to receive questions. When posting closes, MASC will 
send link for drop box for committee to see applicants resumes and experience. 
 
Could make a decision on 31st. Otherwise would want to meet again that week.  Could 
===PAGE 3===
also meet 1st, 2nd, 3rd, 4th.   
 
Not public until School Committee decides, and then publishes.  Question whether 
internal applicants have to apply?  YES 
 
Posting going out nationally, statewide, social media posts, MASC, MASS. 
 
     Ms. Mallon provided her email and personal cell for any questions or concerns. 
 
     4. Public Comment -none

2. Public Comment - Casey McDonnell 1024 Massachusetts  I want to start off tonight by saying I know it's been a lot, 
there's a lot of people that do not envy the position that this board is in. It's definitely easier being on this side whether 
we're coming at it with our own biases or whether we're not.  It's much easier being here nitpicking the decisions and the 
actions that the board is getting. I understand that myself I'm a teacher in the high school I teach History and ELA. I'm 
also very proud to be the head coach of the football program that has been brought up several times by this board in the 
last couple meetings. I put all that aside, I have my own thoughts, I have my own concerns about  things that have been

Mr. Beardmore I've got a few comments tonight. First I'd like to address the point that was made by Mr. Snyder a little 
bit earlier about the transportation budget. Sometimes when you read lines from left to right in a budget they don't  
necessarily reflect the reality of the situation. The cost of athletic transportation is not up 200%. The way we are 
accounting for athletic transportation has been corrected from past practice that was done incorrectly. It was not 
transparent therefore, you are seeing a number that is substantially higher than what was carried in previous years, but is

Ms Brzozoski makes a motion to table the cut to the ski team and give the athletic director an opportunity to 
negotiate terms with co-op schools, Mr. Lehtinen seconds the motion all approve 
 
Ms. Archambault makes a motion to accept the superintendent’s proposal to restructure of the Business 
Finance Director Position, Mr. Lehtinen seconds the motion all approve 
 
Mr. Sculimbrene makes a motion to vote on bus fees and send to policy subcommittee  for setting rates and 
language of safety of travel.  The rates would be $180 for 1 child, $270 for 2 kids or more and a $50 reduced 
fee, Ms. Archambault seconds the motion.  Motion passes 4:1 with Ms. Brzozoski opposing.  
 
Ms. Archambault makes a motion to accept the superintendents recommended budget with the change of the 
ski team continuation to be funded by the bus fee $25,787,474 and 3.6% Mr. Beardmore seconds the motion 
all approve.

disappointed that we waited this long.  I know we were waiting for the state to save us, that wasn’t  going to happen.  I 
had a good meeting with Senator Cronin last week.  I have a meeting coming up Kushmerek and Scarsdale.   I think 
eventually there is going to be something there, but we have to limp through until then.  They are not going to be able to  
fix every problem, so I haven’t come up here with a plan of what to say, but I think we need to start planning in June.  If 
this committee doesn’t think this is the time for the override that is fine.  The only good thing I have really gotten from 
this whole thing is that if you are active on social media, there are many parents in this town who you been getting their 
emails who are incredibly fired up.  I just want to implore the public to stay engaged because you are only governed by 
people who volunteer. It is government, it is not about every single person, and government is about those who show up.  
That is the reality of the way the system works. 
Amy King, 3 Gabes Place I echo what Mr. Santry and what Sandi had to say about the high school taking huge hits 
here.  The ability for our kids to have the experience they need to get into college. I implore the school committee and 
the future school committee that as a finance professional the manner in which we put this budget together and that we 
look for trends for expenses and revenues. Splitting everything into revolving funds is not a good way to manage 
finances.  We need to lay out all revenues and all expenses.  We need to understand what those expenses are, how they 
===PAGE 7===
 
1025 Massachusetts Avenue, Lunenburg, Massachusetts 01462 
978 582-4100 ~ fax 978 582-4103 
increase and decrease and where the funding is coming from so that the public has the full transparency of where all of 
the money is going.  Someone just mentioned the parking fee that we pay doesn’t even go to the school, why not, isn’t it 
because they supposedly plow the parking lot, I mean the $50 parking fee, every one of those kids almost fell in the 
parking lot on the ice this year.  I really think we need to do a better job as everyone has said, in how we are managing 
our finances. I would love to see something done with the curriculum director to get more money into the high school.  I 
think that is a position, that while it is nice have at the state we are in, this school system we need teachers, we need 
offering for these kids.  We have done a great job improving the curriculum over the years.  Can we take a break for a 
few years and give the high school kids a little bit more options. 
Mr. Sculimbrene I think what TJ said is right, we need to have a long-term plan.  I think that one of the things we 
would do as a school committee next year is immediately star working on the long term plan.  One problem I see and I 
keep coming back to is we don’t do this budget discussion correctly.  No one brings forward a case about what impact 
this will have on student achievement.  What I want to know is the dollar value of every impact on student achievement.  
We have people come up and say I love this or that, I don’t want this to go, and my kid does this. But we don’t even 
know how to quantify student achievement in a predictable way that allows us to do a forecast.  We need to have that 
data, we need to have a utilitarian approach because if all we do is constantly listen to people making good cases for 
things.  Everyone loves their children, all of these things are good things, we could make a c ase for any of them, but if 
we don’t have a way of figuring out which ones count more, which ones are worth more, which ones have a bigger 
impact, we are going to perpetually be in a budget cycle that is based on emotion not fact.   First thing we have to do is 
answer TJ’s question is we need to create a rubric to decide budget decisions to make calls about student impact.  The 
second thing I think that we need to do whether it is as a parent or group, we need to understand how legislation changes.  
We are not in the 1700s version of the United States, a citizen request is not going to change the agenda.  I testified two 
weeks ago in New Hampshire, I was the only non-lobbyist in the room and I was a lawyer.  There were no citizens’ there 
at all.  It was a decision that impacted hundreds of millions of dollars.  It was for the insurance, it impacted hundreds of 
millions of dollars, the reason the hearing happened was because people with money coordinated together and got a 
lobbyist, we keep talking about this, we keep saying our legislative delegation isn’t coming to our aid. That is true, but 
we haven’t approached them in a way that people do now.  If we expect the 17 th century version of all of us get on horses 
and ride to Boston to work, it is not going to work.  The last thing I would point out is I understand the concern about not 
going for an override.  I do think that there was a little bit of politics last night in the presentation about why don’t you 
go for an override, when the groundwork that it took to get the override last year was already months underway by this 
point.  You don’t go to the bank and get a loan to start a business in an un-pressed shirt and none of your tax records, we 
need to have that level of preparation when we go and ask for a big amount of money.  We can show the town that we 
have done our homework.  This is the only options and we need it because of the way inflation has impacted municipa l 
finances. My guess is given the people that said we should do it now, they know that we would fai l and cannot fail for 
the kids.  Doing it now at the cost of doing it later better, doesn’t seem the best.  I think that we should as soon as this is 
done, start planning for next year.  We should have a vision and a vision should be based on real concrete information, 
dollars, data, student achievement, we can’t keep doing this on an emotional level  
Ms. Brzozoski I will be honest, I came home from the meeting last night thinking what the heck did I do.  We were not 
going to go for an override, this doesn’t quite sit right with me, because these cuts are so difficult.  But like Tony said w e 
need a comprehensive plan.  I am trying to balance it against what the town is going to need in the future.  The town is 
not ready to present what their needs are going to be.  I said it last night too, I don’t think we are going to get three yea rs 
in a row of overrides, I don’t think Lunenburg tax payers are going to be wild about voting three years in a row for 
overrides. I just don’t.  I wish they were because I think that would be great and I think overrides exist for a reason 
especially when inflation is high.  I don’t think we could sell it and I have to live in the town too.  I would like us to have 
a functioning fire department and the things that would bring that they are going to ask for next year.  It is a calculated 
risk, is it the right answer? There is no right answer because all the options suck.  This is awful, this is terrible and I hate 
it.  I hate that it affects our kids, which makes it terrible.  I wish this was a board that didn’t affect our children, but I 
think this is what we keep saying – the least bad option.  I think we are going to have a rough year, I think that we have 
managed to make a budget that we are going to have a rough year, but it is not going to be a disastrous year.  I think that 
we have built structures in place that we are going get by.  Lunenburg has a very strong staff and teachers th at are going 
to make it work.  We have fantastic staff and teachers, I think we are going to make it work for the year.  I don’t think we 
are going to make it work long-term under this staffing, but I think we can make this work for a year.  Then we are go ing 
to figure this out next year starting soon.  We are going to go up from there and fight for an override next year.   
Mr. Beardmore I am not going to belabor my reasoning that I outlined last night about the override.  I am still very 
comfortable with that reasoning and I understand that you can’t make all the people all the time.  This is a difficult 
decision, I do want to say that these are really difficult challenges I volunteered for the finance committee six years ago.  
I have spent almost three years on the school committee, it is getting harder and harder and frankly I am out of answers.  
Which is part of my decision to leave the school committee at the end of budget season.  I am exhausted by it.  That 
being said what has been represented through the last several weeks by people who have come and spoken and by to 
conversations that we have had here, some of which have been very spirited debates and we have certainly disagreed on 
===PAGE 8===
 
1025 Massachusetts Avenue, Lunenburg, Massachusetts 01462 
978 582-4100 ~ fax 978 582-4103 
some issues is ultimately the way to come to those solutions.  We have some good energy in the community, we have 
good ideas and to the extent that I can I will continue to support the schools. Please keep in mind, while we are governed 
a little bit differently we are a department in the town of Lunenburg and we all live in the town of Lunenburg.  I am not 
particularly happy with the way the town has been run.  I think it is run way too lean, but we have got to give our new 
select board, town manager, and new school committee the opportunity to envision what that is going to look like and 
ideally support them to move them in the right directions.  I am hopeful that the leaders that have stepped up and our new 
leader, and our town manager, and that we can hire a really good leader to help us in the schools and figure this out o ver 
the course of the next several months. 
Ms. Archambault I just want to say that despite all the gray weather her and the blizzard that we are maneuvering 
through, I see a really good silver lining.  The silver lining is the engagement of so many people.   As long as so many 
people stay engaged we will find a way out of these difficult issues.  We have found a way out before and we will find a 
way out of this one.  I want to thank the people for their thoughts and their difficult nights of not sleeping, be cause they 
are worried about this.  I want to thank the staff for hanging in there because having been on that side many times, not 
knowing if my job was going to be cut or not is torturous and I thank them for hanging in there, it is not easy to do. 
Moving forward next year you will get this figured out and I think Tony will still be with you and has committed to 
hanging in there and finding a plan. 
Mr. Lehtinen I want to thank the students, the children of Lunenburg, the parents, and the staff for being c onstantly 
involved and giving feedback.  I want to thank Dr. Burnham and her team for all this work and thank the fellow 
committee members too. I think we did it as a team, we did it as a community, it’s not great, but it is the best we could 
do.  I think having all those voices and emails and public comments made the difference and it gave a full picture of what 
we needed to do and how best we could serve the Lunenburg Public School.  I want to thank everyone for staying 
engaged and going to town meeting, voting, and running for office. Volunteer and be part of the community. 
 
5. Adjournment Ms. Brzozoski makes a motion to adjourn at 9:16 pm, Mr. Beardmore seconds the motion, all 
approve. 
 
 
Respectfully Submitted 
 
 
Courtney Fuller

MEETING MINUTES  
SCHOOL COMMITTEE MEETING  
Wednesday, March 19, 2025 
      LHMS Collaborative Room #D132 
6:30 – Hybrid 
 
School Committee    Superintendent  
Brian Lehtinen, Chair - Present   Dr. Kate Burnham  
Anthony Sculimbrene, Vice Chair - Present 
Carol Archambault, Secretary - Present    
Peter Beardmore, Member - Present 
Laura Brzozoski, Member - Present 
     Recording Secretary 
     Courtney Fuller 
Guests: Tim Santry – LMHS Principal, Norman Yvon – THES Principal, Carla Squier – Primary Principal, Dr. Deborah Bookis – 
Director of Teaching and Learning, Julianna Hanscom – Director of Special Services, Dr. Susan Gilson – Director of Administration 
& Finance, Patty McCarthy – Guillette – Director of Community School Programs, Kevin Bigelow – Athletic Director 
In accordance with the requirements of the Open Meeting Law, please be advised that this meeting is being recorded and broadc ast 
live over the Lunenburg Public Access channel, on Facebook Live on the Public Access Facebook page. 
(http://www.facebook.com/Lunenburg-Public-Access-Cable-157427270975305/) and will be uploaded to the Lunenburg Access 
YouTube after the meeting. 
 
The agenda lists all the topics which may be discussed at the meeting and those reasonably anticipated by the Chair.   Votes may be 
taken as a result of these discussions.  Not all items listed may in fact be discussed and other items not listed may be brought up for 
discussion to the extent permitted by Open Meeting Law 
 
1. Call to Order by Mr. Lehtinen at 6:31 
 
2. Pledge of Allegiance 
 
3. Public Comment – Mandy Gilman, 186 Reservoir Road I have been doing some advocacy work 
with our state legislators about more school funding.  A group of parents and I met with both sets of 
legislatures, Senator John Cronin and Representative Scarsdale and Representative Kushmerek over 
the last few weeks, I would encourage anyone who is listening to this, if you would like to reach out 
to them please send them an email, tell them how devastating the budget cars are four our school 
district.  They are all big supporters of getting more money, but they need to hear from us, they need 
to continue to hear from us, they need to hear from us multiple times.  Even if you have already sent 
them an email, send another one.  It is really important because they have to go to leadership and get 
more support and if they can say they got 100 emails from their constituents that is more convincing.  
There are a few things I want to let people know about.  I know these are during the day, which isn’t 
great if you work a full time job, but I am just letting people know that there is a hearing on Monday 
March 24th at 11 am at UMass Amherst.  This is a hearing only for invited members of state 
government to testify, but it is the education hearing, so there will be a lot of education officials 
there testifying.  I am going to go, I know there are a lot of other groups including North Middlesex 
and Amherst that are having difficulty with funding that are going to show up.  If you want to come 
join our Facebook group or send an email to one of the school committee members. I am happy to 
coordinate with people, if you are not on Facebook.  Number two, there is a public hearing on 
Lunenburg Public Schools 
 
 Dr. Kate Burnham, Superintendent of Schools  

===PAGE 2===
 
1025 Massachusetts Avenue, Lunenburg, Massachusetts 01462 
978 582-4100 ~ fax 978 582-4103 
Tuesday April 8th at 11 am in Boston at the State House.  This hearing is an opportunity to actually 
speak.  I have already been in touch with them, I am going to make some testimony, I am hopeful 
that maybe some other members of this committee or the select board, or the superintendent.  We 
have been coordinating to get people, I think we have some willingness for folks to come and testify.  
These are opportunities for people besides our state legislators, people who are making the budget 
decisions to see the problems with school funding across the state.  Lastly I am organizing a zoom 
call next Wednesday at 8, it is in our Facebook group, if you aren’t on Facebook send an email to the 
school committee members and they can forward it to me.  Something we are going to talk about is 
state advocacy and we are also going to talk about possible override advocacy.  I know that is 
something that is still in the works, for those that are not aware, a group of parents did file an 
override ballot initiative for about $2 million.  I think some decisions need to be made.  I do urge the 
members of the school committee to think about if the select board is thinking about.  I have heard 
through the grapevine that the select board is also considering adding things to the override number, 
if we decide to file, I am not against other department in town getting money, but no one else had 
any problems until the moment the override got filed, so let’s not make it so big that it will not pass. 
Briana Proctor, 141 Gilchrest Street I am a 2011 graduate of Lunenburg High School and I also 
coach girls’ basketball and softball at the High School.  I am here to advocate to save the proposed 
administrative cuts to LMHS.  It is incredibly shortsighted to remove an administrator from the 
building with the largest population of students, who have the most complex challenges as they are 
navigating adolescence.  As a coach at the high school, I see the time the administrators spend 
outside of school hours to help monitor sporting events, a responsibility that administrators at the 
Primary and Elementary schools frankly do not have.  Some of these administrators are current and 
future coaches for the school. Students and athletes whose coaching role would likely be lost, should 
they no longer be employed here.  Administrators’ jobs have become very complex given societal 
changes, and they are an integral part of supporting teachers to create a safe and effective learning 
environment.  To cut a school of around 800 students down to three administrators, while leaving 
smaller schools, with less complex problems fully staffed would harm both the students and the 
teachers at LMHS.  Should this committee not be swayed in the LMHS position not to be a saved, I 
hope that you at least inform the person on the chopping block, so the entirety of the staff is not 
living in a constant state of anxiety. 
Laura Barooshian, 389 Mulpus Road I am here to advocate for the fifth grade music program cuts 
and salary cuts.  The cut to the program is going to be devastating on our program.  The upcoming 
fifth grade program is bigger than it has been in the past five years, it 20% bigger.  The fifth grade 
program feeds directly into the Middle and High school band program.  The kids that have allied 
together, the music program, the marching program have worked incredibly hard.  Multiple studies 
are done on the impact that music has on kids as young as four years old.  Their interpersonal skills, 
their leadership skills, their math, it impacts all academic curriculum.  It is really shortsighted, it is 
very small amounts of money to keep our fifth grade program.  I know you are wrestling a very large 
gap between spending and the budget, and I know it needs to come from somewhere, but I just really 
want you to take a hard look at some of the smaller dollar things that should maybe be used with free 
cash.  I ask you to look at which programs are being maybe inequitably harmed. 
Ms. Brzozoski I have been really uncomfortable since our last meeting with the select board.  The 
decision to wait a year for an override has not been sitting well with me.  As we have continued to 
closely examine the cuts it has been more and more painful and it has been getting more and more 
uncomfortable.  It makes sense to wait from a holistic and a strategy view, and I see the Town 
Manager and the Select Boards reasoning.  There is no way they can get the override numbers 
together for a holistic override this year.  I understand the reasons why, but I cannot stop thinking 
since our meeting yesterday is that we weren’t elected to represent the interest of the whole town.  
We are the school committee, and it is our job, our duty to fight for the students and what the 
===PAGE 3===
 
1025 Massachusetts Avenue, Lunenburg, Massachusetts 01462 
978 582-4100 ~ fax 978 582-4103 
students need.  I am not sure we are doing that duty if we wait another year.  My motivation was to 
hold out for the free cash, but that money is not guaranteed and the amount keeps going down. There 
was a discussion about putting money into the paving, they don’t want to reduce that amount while I 
am sitting watching and we are laying off 10% of our staff. I said it last week and I am going to say 
it again, these are the kids that have lived through covid, they have not had a normal childhood, 
they’ve already had to go through so much.  Why are we in Lunenburg, insisting on making things 
harder for our kids?  Why can’t we give them the basics of a quality education? I can’t understand 
why we are choosing to do this to our students.  I have faith in our staff and teachers and I think we 
can make it through a rough year, but I can’t understand why we are doing it when we have other 
options.  I want people who don’t have kids to understand what an entire year of child’s life is 
developmentally, how much they grow and change.  How much teachers are trying to cram in their 
head in a single year.  I don’t think people who do not have kids are understanding what waiting a 
year means for a kid in first grade. It means a kid in high school isn’t getting to take a class they 
need before graduation, it means students are missing out on supports they need to reach their full 
potentials as humans, as adults, which is our end goal.  Last night at the select board meeting they 
did discuss going for a town wide override now that there is a citizen’s petition for a school wide 
override.  I don’t understand why the urgency now?  Back then we were still cutting 10% of the staff 
and looking at class sizes of potentially 30 kids, facing these devastating cuts and it wasn’t met with 
urgency then. I am grateful there is a citizens override on the ballot, I am grateful there is hopefully 
going to be an option to vote for it and I am going to fight for it. 
Mr. Beardmore I share all of the concerns that Laura spoke of, however, my position on the 
override has not changed. I said that we need to go for a town wide, a well-considered and well 
vetted town wide override next year.  That being said if a school override lands on the town meeting 
warrant and the ballot for this year, I will absolutely support it and help to whatever means that I am 
able to.  I spoke to the chair of the select board earlier today and I am very concerned that the plan 
seems to be an influx as a result of the citizens’ petition.  I am concerned the new numbers are going 
to brought from the town and have not been vetted to any extent.  I am very concerned this may end 
being a poison pill.  I don’t know that is the intent, but it certainly may be the intent it could be the 
effect and I just want to voice my concern. Certainly for all of the reasons that Laura stated if this 
does end up on the warrant, and ballot, I do intend to support it. 
Ms. Archambault I am planning on attending the March 24 meeting with the Ways and Means 
committee in Amherst.  If anyone has letters or concerns about our budget, this is the time to do it I 
have reached out to three legislators and heard from three of legislators stating that they are going to 
push what they can to get additional funding for our schools in legislative session.  I have also 
reached out to the chairperson of the education committee in the Senate as well as in the House and I 
suggest everybody do that, the more letters they get the more they will pay attention to what we 
need.  I have never been to a hearing like this before so I’m quite curious about it.  I am happy to go 
and will bring my thoughts.  I have no idea if I will have a chance to voice them or share them, but I 
will bring our thoughts that we are getting crushed here and that the state needs to do something. 
John Bowen Jr. 162 Highland Street I gave this speech last night at the select board meeting but I 
am going to give it here to.  I am speaking tonight about Lunenburg schools I recently moved here to 
Lunenburg with my dad, John Bowen, in January of last year because I was in a bad environment 
which caused me a lot of difficulties in my life mentally, emotionally, socially, and academically. 
Every school I attended which was three last year alone and seven schools over five years was a real 
challenge. I had no support from people I was living with and I was abused, not physically but 
mentally and was told my father didn't love me. I did not know that my father was in a bitter legal 
fight for more than 10 years all of this was before I came to Lunenburg to live with my dad. In my 
previous living situation I was told I should know what to do with homework assignments and with 
regular life skills. This was my whole life and I felt trapped, whenever I got upset because I was 
===PAGE 4===
 
1025 Massachusetts Avenue, Lunenburg, Massachusetts 01462 
978 582-4100 ~ fax 978 582-4103 
frustrated with understanding everything. My mother would force me at the four hospitals between 
the ages of 9 and 15 all the time telling me I am the problem and I am no different than my father 
which she characterized him in a negative light to me and my sister daily. However, I am speaking 
with you today because of my father, and because of Lunenburg High School. I never thought I 
could do this a year ago. Why am I sharing this with you?  My father has shown me having one 
voice and one vote is important!  That I should always speak up this is how I heal from my traumatic 
path I am who I am today by speaking up and speaking out.  I want you to know that because of 
people here in Lunenburg, my dad, of course, my stepmom Jean Marie Price, and from school 
Joshua Koziol, Juliana Hanscom, Lori Shea, James Sweeney, Coach Hausler, Jennifer Miller, Stacey 
Canfield, Timothy Santry, and all my other teachers and mentors and friends at Lunenburg High 
School including Mr. and Mrs. Eberhardt, Mrs. Foyle, and Mrs. Maillet and all my friends and the 
entire class of 2027 for voting me to ride the float with Kendra Eberhardt at homecoming this was a 
great honor. There are so many others I want to name just not enough time, all of these people have 
helped this person, me, who stands before you today who has had an IEP his whole life and today I 
do not have any special needs requirements because of a good plan that Lunenburg High School 
formulated for me and working with my father. Since coming to Lunenburg high, my first semester 
here I made honors, my second semester here I made High honors, in this past semester I made 
special honors, and it appears I am keeping the ship steady. This is because of the program that 
Lunenburg high school has for kids with special needs including kids like me who come from 
difficult homes or abusive homes. I have excelled because of the special needs assistance and the 
professionalism of how Lunenburg High School worked with my father and with me. They helped 
me make my life better academically and socially. I have failed every grade since seventh grade and 
I was just passed along by other schools. I thought no one cared however, not today, today I have a 
direction, I am planning on law school and then the Marine Corps. I also intend to run for president 
of the class of 2027 at Lunenburg high school and I will be looking for signatures.  What I'm really 
here today to say to you is, you have difficult decisions to make about the schools but the one thing 
you should support 100% is the special services needed for all the kids in Lunenburg schools. I play 
sports and I love sports but if you cut that I am okay with it happening. If you have to cut any other 
after school activity I will be ok with that too.  I just want to be heard that cutting any program or 
assistance for people with educational or social difficulties, maybe some with my situation of an 
abusive home life with no support, as a  survivor myself,  I know there are many out there and you 
cannot have them slip through the cracks.   Cutting special needs education or the professionals’ 
attached to the education in Lunenburg schools is like removing life support from a patient who 
can’t live without the assistance. 
Mr Sculimbrene I just want to say that was an amazing speech. 
Ms. Brzozoski I just want to follow up on Carol’s comments.  If you ware writing letters to our 
elected officials, they should have your address, your full address and the address where you are 
registered to vote at the bottom. It makes it more official for them. 
 
4. Chair’s Report The select board is meeting at Town Hall in 7 minutes.  They are discussing the FY 
25 budget, FY26 budget capital plan and annual town meeting warrant articles and most likely will 
be discussing the override.  State House news service this morning stated that two weeks before the 
policy is set to expire, House democrats moved Monday to temporarily extend pandemic era laws 
allowing public meetings to offer remote access.  The House Ways and Means Committee began 
advancing a bill that would keep in place the language granting public bodies flexibility to hold 
meetings virtually or in hybrid formats, as well as measures lowering the number of people 
necessary for a quorum at Town Meeting, and allowing representative town meetings to be held with 
remote participation.  Those policies are set to expire March 31st the house bill would push that 
sunset date to June 30, 2027.  There is a citizen’s petition for an override that was submitted by TJ 
===PAGE 5===
 
1025 Massachusetts Avenue, Lunenburg, Massachusetts 01462 
978 582-4100 ~ fax 978 582-4103 
Loughlin signed by many citizens, the language of the warrant article to see if the town will vote to 
raise and appropriate $2,099,337 to supplement Lunenburg Public Schools budget for fiscal year 
2026 contingent upon the passage of a proposition 2 ½ ballot questions.  The number was calculated 
by the difference between the level service budget and the town managers budget.  Additionally the 
Joint Committee on Ways and Means FY26 public hearing is on Tuesday April 8, 2025, the hearing 
begins at 11 am at the State House.  You can join educators and public education supporters from 
across the Commonwealth as they advocate for the FY26 funding for schools, colleges, and 
universities.  The MTA is also organizing this, but they are looking for written testimony and we 
will be talking about later also.

discussion to the extent permitted by Open Meeting Law 
 
1. Call to Order by Mr. Lehtinen at 6:31

3. Public Comment – Carol Archambault This afternoon the House Ways and Means Committee did 
submit their budget.  Their budget has a minimum aid to school raising from the $75 per student to 
$150, however, this is a huge example of how you can’t count on anything because this has to go to 
the Senate, so right now it looks like we would have more Chapter 70 funding.  However it could be 
cut in half come July 1st, it is very important for people to realize these things go up and down.  
There is also potential for 75% of all required out of district placement eligible for reimbursement, 
which again could change and cannot be counted on. 
 
Peter Beardmore There have been a number public hearings over the course of the last several 
weeks pertaining to education funding.  There was a hearing in Boston last week that I know the 
superintendent and two of our members attended.  I was able to watch some of both the hearing in 
Springfield and the hearing in Boston over the internet.  I recognized a lot of impassioned testimony 
on the part of parents, students, municipal leaders, and quite frankly what I consider to be a lot of 
political theater on the part of acting commissioner of education and many legislators.  What came 
out last week in the way of a proposal that is explained as a one-time funding from the fair share tax.  
Although the fair share tax is a permanent tax the funding proposal is one time funding which I don’t 
quite understand.  As well as a House budget I have inquired of our legislators relative to the bill that 
came out last week, what the net effect on Lunenburg? I would encourage people to repeat and 
Lunenburg Public Schools 
 
 Dr. Kate Burnham, Superintendent of Schools  

===PAGE 2===
 
1025 Massachusetts Avenue, Lunenburg, Massachusetts 01462 
978 582-4100 ~ fax 978 582-4103 
reprint to their hearts content because we need that number and while all money at any time is 
sufficient, the sooner the better 
 
4. Chair’s Report I also have the House and Ways Means Committee was expected to release their 
FY26 budget today prompting increased advocacy efforts as state budget activities.  The state house 
news service reported that the speaker Mariano’s comments at a Boston Chamber of Commerce 
event last week was Mariano confirmed that the House Ways and Means budget will sustain funding 
for universal free school meals and fully support the Student Opportunity Act, however he ruled out 
reforming the Chapter 70 school funding despite calls from superintendents, school committees, and 
students at budget hearings. When asked post event about revising the formula, a topic Senate 
president Karen Spilka had shown interest in, Mariano firmly declined stating , no too much 
uncertainty. Representatives will have until Friday to submit amendments and then we encourage the 
public to contact your district’s representatives this week and urge them to file amendments 
establishing a chapter 70 commission using the language from Senator Lewis’s bill Senate 400 and 
all requests outlined in the FY26 advocacy document.  The 211 have been working from 
Representative Scarsdale house bill H405 was finalized as H410.  An act making appropriations for 
fiscal year 2025 to provide for supplementing certain existing appropriations and for certain other 
activities and projects.  It allocates a one-time 1.3 billion FY24 fair share amendments tax surplus 
and has been referred to the Senate.  Key provisions to the bill were a full 100% reimbursement for 
FY25 regional transportation up from 82%, a 75% reimbursement increased from 44% for FY out of 
district special education transportation cost through circuit breaker, a directive for the office of 
Inspector General to review transportation and recommendations by February 2, 2026 to reduce 
costs and enhance purchasing power and streamline services.  Also Representative Kushmerek and 
Scarsdale reached out to me stating that there are $50,000 in earmarks for Lunenburg Public 
Schools.  The chair of the select board also reached out stating the same. 
 
5. Student Representative Report In the Middle High School they have been having the spring food 
drive, I have seen a lot of kinds bringing things in.  We have the Spain trip leaving tomorrow and the 
DC trip is happening next month.  We have had MCAS in the schools.  Elementary school is having 
field day on June 11th and the Primary School will have theirs on June 12.  The fifth grade will have 
their promotion ceremony is June 13 
 
6. Review & Approve Minutes 
 
a. Regular Session 2/26/25 Mr. Sculimbrene make a motion to approve the minutes, Ms. 
Archambault seconds the motion, all approved via roll call vote 
 
7. Review & Approve Warrants, Line Item Transfers & Donations 
a. Warrants 3/27/25 to 4/14/25 $368,097.31 
b. Donations A donation in the amount of $800 for the Parilla family for STEM supplies for the 
Primary School and will be deposited into the gift account for future STEM supplies.  We 
also have a check for $1000 from an anonymous donor to help students going to Nature’s 
Classroom in October 2025 that check will be deposited into the gift account at Turkey Hill 
Elementary.  Mr. Beardmore makes a motion to accept both donations with gratitude, 
Ms. Archambault seconds the motion all approve via roll call 
c. Line Item Transfer A transfer in the amount of $19,600 from computer purchase and lease 
line to the equipment maintenance and lease line, this is for an anticipated expense from 
Canon. Second line item transfer request of $5,000 moving from the fire safety furniture 
repair line to the vehicle maintenance repair line, this transfer will cover the remaining 
===PAGE 3===
 
1025 Massachusetts Avenue, Lunenburg, Massachusetts 01462 
978 582-4100 ~ fax 978 582-4103 
repairs to the truck and mower for the remainder of the year. A third transfer from the school 
salary reserve line of $311,041.69 to the multiple salary lines. A fourth transfer to move 
funds to the athletic transportation, special detail, dues and fees and the high school after 
school stipends, this is the first phase move making monies available in the athletic portion of 
the budget.  We can go back and reclassify expenses that had been charged against the 
revolving account. Mr. Beardmore makes a motion to approve the line item transfers, 
Ms. Archambault seconds the motion all approve via roll call. (Ms. Brzozoski was 
unavailable for this vote) 
 
8. Superintendents Report The transportation form were a little late getting out to families, we 
weren’t able to get the payment option on revtrak up and running in time.  Transportation forms are 
due by May 1st, at this point we have only received 413 forms.  You do not have to fill out a bus 
waiver form if you have already submitted a free and reduced application and qualify, because we 
will be relying on that information for the bus waiver.  The free and reduced applications can be 
found on our website on the main page and on the transportation page, for anyone submitted an 
application, you will also need to complete and submit a sharing form also available on our website.  
In order to pay the bus fee, you can send a check made out to Town of Lunenburg and send it to The 
Central Office 1025 Massachusetts Ave, make sure to put bus fee in the memo section of the check. 
Once revtrak is open and operational we will send out a notification to families.  Additional details 
about the bus fee can be found on the website on the transportation page. To date we have received 
27 bus waiver requests.  Moving forward payment will be due at the time you fill out the 
transportation form.  We have extended a due date for payment to June 1st.  Registration for the 
summer program extended day will open April 28.  Before and after school program for the fall will 
open May 5th and Early Learning Center Program will open May 19th.  We have been notified that 
we were awarded the innovation grant from Innovation Land Education, this was one of the 
stackable grants.  Innovation Land will work with us providing us letters of support for additional 
grants that we should pursue in order to complement their grant.  Our current plan is to apply for 
both the Ryman Foundation grant and the SC Johnson grant both of which have rolling application 
periods.  Routinely our tech department repairs broken Chromebooks, replaces screens and we were 
out of available parts, we had to switch vendors and the prices went up 54% due to tariffs, I think we 
need to be prepared it is going to be a bumpy ride relative to our funding in our current approved 
budget.  Town meeting, our food service program will be offering bagged lunches, cost will be $8 
and be available between 11 and 1, cash and carry only.  I was one of those in attendance at the 
Ways and Means hearing on April 8th, I did submit written testimony advocating for some of the 
bills that are under consideration at the moment, and based on estimates for revenues that could 
come to Lunenburg if those were passed.   We should be able to restore 9 positions if all of them 
pass. 
Upcoming Dates 
April 21 Schools and Offices are closed for the Holiday 
April 22 -25 Schools are closed for April Vacation 
May 7 School Committee meeting 6:30 Town Hall 
 
 
9. Presentation LMBDC Mr. Michael Ray Jefferies gives a presentation on updates that the Municipal 
Building Design Company has been working on which includes The Ritter Building, Town Hall, and 
Meeting House.  The presentation that will be shared at Town Meeting is shared with the committee.

10. Old Business 
        Discussion 
a. Student Activity Fee Restructure LMHS The committee has been provided an update in 
their folder regarding the progress that has been made thus far. 
b. Athletic Summer Camp Update Dr. Burnham makes the committee aware that our AD 
Bigelow has accepted a new position, he will be resigning effective June 30.  We did reach 
out to the Board of Health there is a rather lengthy check list of tasks to take care of in order 
to be compliant.  Given the short timeline I am not confident at this point that we are going to 
be able to satisfy all of the Board of Health requirements. They are working on the list, and 
while we may not be able to hold camps this summer, I think it is worthwhile to continue the 
progress with paperwork so long term we can pursue this as an option. 
c. School Committee Goals passed over 
Action 
d. THES ADA Study Report Dr. Gilson introduces the architect Kaja to the committee and 
shares a presentation.  They are wrapping up the study of conditions and assessments and 
ADA compliance for Turkey Hill. Kaja shares the details of parts of the building that have 
been assessed and what would be recommended for replacement or repair.  The engineer also 
shares information on scenarios and costs. Ms. Brzozoski makes a motion to accept the 
report as a draft, Mr. Beardmore seconds the motion all approve 
e. FY26 Budget – Free Cash & FinCom memo The committee discusses the FY 26 budget, 
position cuts, unemployment costs. We have developed a memo to be shared with the finance

printed and available to the public and at town meeting Mr. Beardmore makes a motion to

motion all approve.  Mr. Brzozoski makes a motion to move forward with the 
recommendations for free cash, Mr. Sculimbrene seconds the motion all approve 
f. School Choice Dr. Burnham shares options of opening school choice beyond 9th grade.  Dr. 
Burnham goes through each grade, their size, and potential seats to open.  Mr. Beardmore 
makes a motion to accept the proposal, contingent on the passing of free cash, Mr. 
Sculimbrene seconds the motion 
g. Warrant Articles Mr. Lehtinen goes through the warrant article 7 and how it relates to the 
school Mr. Beardmore makes a motion to support Article 7, Mr. Sculimbrene seconds 
the motion all in favor.

support article 9, Ms. Brzozoski seconds the motion, all approve 
Article 11 is the citizens petition for the override –  no motion 
Article 12 Supplement LPS fiscal year operating budget Mr. Beardmore makes a motion to 
support article 12, Mr. Sculimbrene seconds the motion all approve 
Article 28 Marshall Park Design Debt Exclusion, the Parks Commission shares details of the 
plan  Ms. Archambault makes a motion to support article 28, Ms. Brzozoski seconds the 
motion, the motion passes 4 -1 with Mr. Beardmore voting no 
Article 29 LMBDC debt exclusion Mr. Beardmore makes a motion to support article 29, 
Ms. Archambault seconds the motion, motion passes 4-1 with Mr. Sculimbrene voting

12. Public Comment Chris Sullivan 108 Island Street As a private citizen, listening earlier in terms of 
the athletic director discussion,.  The possible solution that was proposed budgetary and totally get 
where we are at budgetary right now obviously, I would hope that we will look at this as a very 
temporary solution.  The reason we have a full time athletic director is we have 50% of the student 
body participating in high school sports.  To think that job can be done on a part time basis is short 
sighted, even in the short term.  Again I understand where we are at and I think we have made a lot 
of strides in the last few years with Lunenburg Athletics in terms of quality of the program and that 
we continue to put out in terms of the level of participation, spectator participation at a lot of our 
sports.  I would just hope this is a necessary short term solution.  Additionally I would just point out 
prior discussions in term s of what our athletics has been charged with, one team in particular, with 
the ski team, I want to make sure we do not lose sight of that discussion that needs to happen with 
the other schools involved and if that is something that is going to default to someone who is part 
time.

Respectfully Submitted 
 
 
Courtney Fuller

live over the Lunenburg Public Access channel, on Facebook Live on the Public Access Facebook page. 
(http://www.facebook.com/Lunenburg-Public-Access-Cable-157427270975305/) and will be uploaded to the Lunenburg Access 
YouTube after the meeting. 
 
The agenda lists all the topics which may be discussed at the meeting and those reasonably anticipated by the Chair.   Votes may be 
taken as a result of these discussions.  Not all items listed may in fact be discussed and other items not listed may be brought up for 
discussion to the extent permitted by Open Meeting Law 
 
1. Call to Order by Mr. Lehtinen at 7:30 pm 
 
2. New Business 
 
a. Finalist Candidate Interview for Superintendent of Schools – Joanne Menard The 
candidate introduces themselves as well as an opening statement.  The committee takes turns 
asking a series of up to 10 questions that have been predetermined and will be the same for 
each candidate.  Each interview will be a max of 75 minutes. During the questions the 
committee and finalist share back and forth conversation related to each specific question.  
At the end of the questions the finalist delivers a closing statement.  The interview recording 
is available online.  Surveys have been sent out to stakeholders to share their input 
 
3. Adjournment Mr. Sculimbrene makes a motion to adjourn at 8:48, Ms. Brzozoski seconds the 
motion, all approve via roll call vote 
 
Respectfully Submitted 
 
 
Courtney Fuller 
 
Lunenburg Public Schools 
 
 Dr. Kate Burnham, Superintendent of Schools

3. Recognition & Farewell to Outgoing Committee Members Mr. Lehtinen reports Mr. Beardmore 
will not be returning, he brought a sharp mind and strategic lens to our work on the school 
committee, his financial expertise and deep understanding of systems made him an invaluable 
resource especially in times when we needed to make tough long- term decisions.  Peter always had 
ideas worth hearing and opinions worth considering.  He never hesitated to speak up even when it 
wasn’t easy or popular.  Sometimes his candor came across a little strong but it always came from a 
place of passion for doing what is right for students and taxpayers.  His honesty and drive to improve 
things and his commitment to transparency have shaped many of the conversations and decisions we 
have had at the table.  Peter’s decision to step away to spend more time with his family speaks 
volumes about his priorities and I know I speak for many when I say we wish him all the best.  His 
contributions to this committee will have a long lasting impact and his voice will certainly be 
missed, but knowing his voice will continue to be heard from the public comment mic. 
Ms. Archambault was already a dedicated member of the school committee when I first joined and 
from the beginning she set an example of thoughtful student centered leadership.  Carol has always 
brought a caring grounded perspective to our work especially when it comes to advocating for our 
youngest learners.  She often saw angles I hadn’t considered and was always willing to dig in to help 
and do the work that often goes unseen, but is so critical.  I have learned a great deal from Carol, not 
only about educational policy, finance, and governance, but how to lead with kindness and clarity.  I

4. Public Comment Justin Repucci 1147 Mass Ave I am going to respectfully ask the school 
committee to consider how the new bus fee policy applies in cases where walking to school is 
unsafe.  We live within the 2 mile range, but we are on a busy state road with no sidewalks.  
Walking is not a safe or a realistic option, I understand the goal of the policy is that distance is used 
as a fair metric, but assumes walking is a viable option, in some cases it is not.  I am not here to ask 
for special treatment based on finances or convenience, I am asking for a process that accounts for 
safety and road conditions when determining who pays for the bus fee.  I’d like to ask the committee 
to consider a safety exemption or review this process so families like mine aren’t penalized for this 
circumstance.

Peter Beardmore I would like to comment on the discussion last night that occurred at the select 
board meeting pertaining to the discussion around the special election.  To complete the override 
process prior to last night’s interview, Laura and I spoke at public comment.  Select board meeting, 
the gist of my public comment was that I think there is going to be additional public outcry to make 
this happen.  Town meeting appropriated the money, the same body that appropriates an omnibus 
budget.  Town meeting appropriated that money, subject to the passage of an override vote.  While 
the select board pays regular lip service to the votes at town meeting, for some reason this  particular 
vote did not meet their muster for affecting their decision to put this on a allot.  I say this, having 
been part of the process where we as a committee came forward and agreed that the less bad course 
of action once again was probably to do this as a town override.  But there is a process and the public 
is included in the process.  There was a citizen petition, and lo and behold that citizen petition held 
the day on Saturday and those who chose to come to town meeting and to vote, voted for it.  An 
appropriation that has been made, but there is no revenue to support that appropriation because the 
public is not being given the opportunity to vote to approve that revenue.  That is the mechanics of 
the situation that we have right now.  The situation is different from the one that existed 2 months 
ago, when we met with the select board.  In deliberations last night, the select board ignored that 
reality.  I think to a certain degree it serves them to make dispersion of the school committee and the 
school budget when frankly our budget has been scrutinized ten times more than any other 
department in Luneburg.  There have been discussions around the forecasting and the long term 
projections of the costs associated with running the school department to a much greater degree than 
any other department in town.  There is a blocking mechanism that two of the three members that 
voted against or spoke against this last night used a year ago in opposing the override.  Here we sit, 
nothing has been done, one of those members is leaving and I don’t know what to make of it.  I am 
exasperated by a board that claims to represent the will of the people of Lunenburg, but will not put 
it to a vote.  I still believe that this is going to happen, it is just a matter of how long we are 
prolonging the agony.  I think there is going to be a special election, the question is, how long the 
select board is going to prolong this agony and the longer it takes the more disruptive it will be to the 
school department operations over the course of the summer and fall. 
Ms. Brzozoski I would like to echo what Peter said.  I also made a public comment not knowing that 
Peter was coming in hot too. What I asked for was for the select board to choose either –act 
immediately on bringing an override for FY26 as a compromise if they are not inclined to take 
action now then to start work toward an FY27 override at special town meeting in November.  This 
way everyone knows what money is coming in when we are doing our budgets.  Making 10 budgets 
is incredibly difficult and it wastes a lot of resources. My main reason is because of what we are 
doing to our staff, when we prolong this agony.  June 30th we have a significant number of staff 
being laid off, we are going to pay out unemployment, and they are going to hopefully and very 
likely end up finding employment in other districts.  These are quality teachers we are losing and it 
hurts every time we do this, it damages morale in the school system, it stresses out our staff.  We are 
not good employers, it is very upsetting what we are doing to our staff, and this is not good 
stewardship.  If we wait until next spring for yet another budget season like this, that will be three 
years in a row that we have put Lunenburg school district staff through this and this is not okay.  I 
am having a difficult time with the fact that the select board is not understanding what this is doing
